# Device-encoder export (WebCodecs fast path)

## Why

The FFmpeg.wasm export decodes and encodes every frame in WebAssembly. On a phone that is the dominant cost: a 3 min 28 s
project took about two hours. The fast path decodes with the device's own video decoder, composites on a canvas and encodes
with the device's own H.264 encoder (hardware where the phone has it). FFmpeg is still used for sound and for the final MP4,
which is cheap work.

**No speed-up has been measured on a phone yet.** Do not quote one until it has been (see "Measuring on a device").

## How it works (`mediay-fast.js`, wired in at `tryDeviceEncoderExport` in `app.js`)

1. `demux()` reads the MP4/MOV `moov` box and builds the sample table. Sample bytes are read later in ranges, so a large file
   is never loaded whole.
2. For each clip a `VideoDecoder` is started at the key frame at or before the trim point. Decoded frames are picked per output
   slot with the same rule as FFmpeg's `fps` filter (rounding to nearest, ties to the later slot), which also handles speed
   changes and 60 -> 30 fps.
3. Each frame is drawn on a canvas (letterbox like `scale+pad`, rotation metadata applied, fade), then caption/sticker overlays
   are drawn for their timeline window. Overlay PNGs come from the existing `overlayPNG()` and are cropped to their visible
   pixels first, so many captions cost little memory.
4. The timeline is divided on output-frame boundaries into short pieces. The maximum piece duration is derived from the
   existing 32 MiB in-memory sink budget and the estimated output bitrate (capped at 30 seconds). Each piece gets a fresh
   encoder session and begins with a keyframe; its Annex B stream is immediately muxed into a small MP4 segment and the raw
   temporary stream is deleted before the next piece is rendered. Clip fades are applied only at the original clip edges.
5. FFmpeg extracts each original clip's sound once (trim, speed, volume, fade, padded to the accumulated frame duration) as WAV.
   A concat-demuxer stream-copy pass joins the video segments and audio pieces into `joined.mp4`; the normal audio-mix and
   save steps follow unchanged. This avoids a single multi-minute JS output buffer and avoids re-encoding during final assembly.

## When it is used

Used only when all of these hold; otherwise the FFmpeg-only path runs exactly as before:

- the export-sheet checkbox "Use this phone's video encoder" is on (default on);
- no clip is reversed, has a filter, colour adjustments or a transition; no overlay is a video or uses a blend mode;
  playback speed is between 0.5x and 2x;
- every source is a plain (non-fragmented), 8-bit MP4/MOV with H.264/HEVC/VP9 video that this device can decode;
- either all clips have a sound track or none do;
- the device accepts an H.264 encoder configuration at the export size and its 12-frame self-test runs at 2 fps or better.

If anything fails after it has started (decoder stall, encoder error, FFmpeg error, out of memory) it removes its files, restores
the progress handler and returns to the FFmpeg-only path. Each await in the render loop has a 30 s watchdog. The Export pane's
performance report records `deviceEncoder: used (...)` or `not used: <reason>` plus the encoder self-test result.

## Known limits

- The browser pipeline runs its deterministic frame-accuracy matrix with VP9 and also exercises the actual H.264 WebCodecs
  encoder/Annex B output when the installed Chromium build exposes it (`tests/fast_pipeline.py`). This is desktop CI coverage,
  not proof that a particular Android WebView exposes a working or fast hardware H.264 encoder; that must still be checked on a phone.
- Each individual encoded piece is bounded by the existing 32 MiB sink budget. FFmpeg.wasm's virtual filesystem still retains the muxed segments until final assembly, so total project size is not a hard 32 MiB limit but total output bytes still consume browser memory.
- Filters, colour adjustments, transitions, reverse, video overlays and blend modes still use the FFmpeg-only path (slow).
  Porting the filters to canvas, or a native Android encoder, are the next steps if those matter.
- Sound from a clip is extracted by FFmpeg from a copy of the source in its virtual filesystem (same cost as before). Extracting
  AAC straight from the file would avoid that copy.
- 10-bit/HDR and fragmented MP4 sources are declined.

## Tests

- `node --test tests/fast.test.js` - parser vs ffprobe (B-frames, moov at end/start), rotation, Annex B conversion decoded
  by ffmpeg, planning maths, unsupported inputs. Needs ffmpeg for the media cases.
- `python tests/fast_pipeline.py` - real browser: frame-accurate trim, speed, fps change, two-clip join, overlay timing,
  letterbox, rotation vs ffmpeg autorotate, fade, cancel.
- `python tests/fast_integration.py` - runs the real `tryDeviceEncoderExport` text from `app.js` against stand-ins and runs the
  FFmpeg commands it produces with a real ffmpeg; checks every decline reason and clean-up on failure.

## Measuring on a device

Export the same project twice, once with the checkbox on and once off, at the same resolution and fps, and copy the performance
report (Export pane -> Performance diagnostics) for each. Record device model, chipset, RAM, source resolution/codec and the
`deviceEncoder` line. Only then state a speed-up.


## Encoder self-test safety

The device encoder capability check has a shared 15-second deadline covering queue drain and encoder flush. If either stalls, the probe rejects, closes the encoder in a `finally` block, and lets the normal FFmpeg export path continue. This is a safety timeout, not a performance guarantee: actual speed and hardware support must still be verified on the target Android device.

FILE: docs/performance-triage.md
BLOB SHA: b7537dc25729533882f7d242632edbc54de46ce8
================================================================================
# MediaY Performance Triage and Next-Agent Handoff

## Current branch changes

This branch adds:
- **Fast draft export**: one click selects 480p / 24 fps. The regular resolution and frame-rate controls remain available for final output.
- **Bounded FFmpeg source cache**: source media assets are reused across repeated clip edits within an export, with a 96 MiB cache budget. Assets too large to cache are transient; the intent is to reduce repeated reads/writes without unbounded WebAssembly memory retention.
- **Bounded overlay PNG cache**: reusable image/text overlay renders are cached within an export, with a 12 MiB budget. Active assets are pinned while an encode pass is using them.
- **FFmpeg workspace cleanup ledger**: known generated input/output files are cleaned on normal completion and recoverable failures.
- **Chunked automatic captions**: transcription inference is issued in 30-second chunks and reports progress at each completed chunk.
- **Caption cancellation and transactional apply**: cancellation is honored between inference/object-build steps; draft captions are only committed after inference finishes. A cancelled inference should not leave a partial caption set.
- **Bounded audio decode reuse**: one decoded audio buffer is reused at most per caption operation, with channel downmixing and 16 kHz resampling when needed.
- **Silent-source skip**: very-low-energy audio tracks are skipped before inference.
- **Batched canvas work**: caption text objects are built without restacking after each item; the layer stack is recalculated once per batch.

## What is verified

On the current feature branch:
- JavaScript syntax check passed.
- Browser smoke tests passed, including trim/undo/redo regressions, fast preset settings, 30-second ASR chunk boundaries, caption source-time offsets, cancel-without-partial-drafts, and silent-audio skip.
- The FFmpeg worker integration smoke test passed.
- Android debug APK build passed.

These are correctness gates, not performance benchmarks. Do not claim a quantified speedup yet; measure representative media on a real device.

## Performance reports available in the app

The Export pane now contains **Performance diagnostics** and **Copy performance report**. The report captures the latest export and caption operation in the current app session. It contains timestamps, settings/counts, stage wall times, and FFmpeg command categories; it intentionally excludes media filenames and media content.

For export, compare preflight/media validation, FFmpeg initialization, asset staging + clip encoding, transition rendering, segment joining, audio mixing, output saving, and cleanup. FFmpeg command time categories are a subset of the stage totals, not additive extra time. For captions, compare model/runtime load, audio decode/resampling, inference, caption overlay creation/commit, and resource cleanup.

On captions, the busy progress dialog now exposes **Request cancellation** too; the previous button lived behind the blocking dialog and could not be clicked during generation. Cancellation is cooperative: it is honored when the current model/audio operation or inference chunk returns. It cannot instantly interrupt an in-flight WASM/GPU inference or the model download.

## Why export can still be slow

MediaY's export path uses FFmpeg WebAssembly in a worker. It encodes main clips individually, applies filters/overlays during each encode, assembles clips, then may mix separate audio tracks. This is substantially more work than a single simple transcode, and repeated clips, many overlays, transitions, high frame rates, and high output resolutions all add cost.

The cache avoids redundant work for repeated source IDs and reusable overlays, but unique large files still need to be copied into FFmpeg's virtual filesystem. The regular export remains a quality-oriented preset; use Fast draft (480p / 24 fps) to validate timing and structure quickly.

Official FFmpeg.wasm performance notes: https://ffmpegwasm.netlify.app/docs/performance/
Official FFmpeg.wasm threading requirements: https://ffmpegwasm.netlify.app/docs/getting-started/usage/#multi-thread
Threaded WebAssembly requires `SharedArrayBuffer` and cross-origin isolation. It is not safe to switch to the multi-thread core until the website host and Android WebView paths can satisfy the required headers and pass memory tests.

## Why automatic captions can still be slow

Captions currently rely on a Transformers.js Whisper-tiny pipeline. The first run downloads a speech model; WebGPU is attempted when the browser advertises it, with a compatibility fallback. Each source still passes through browser `decodeAudioData` for its full encoded media buffer before PCM slicing. That full-file decode and the first model download may dominate time for long source videos.

Chunked inference improves progress reporting, cancellation points, and inference task size; it does **not** guarantee lower total inference time and does not solve whole-file decode latency. Do not hide that distinction in the UI.

Transformers.js model/runtime documentation: https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers.js/guides/webgpu
ASR chunking parameters: https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers.js/api/pipelines#automaticspeechrecognitionspecificparams

## Prioritized work list for the next AI agent

### P0 — measure before changing more knobs
1. Add stage timings around source validation, FFmpeg initialization, input-file staging, each clip encode, transition assembly, audio mixing, and output writing. Show the totals in a diagnostics panel or copyable report.
2. Benchmark the same 30-second and 3-minute test assets at 480p/24, 720p/30, and 1080p/30 on desktop Chromium and a physical Android device. Capture wall-clock time, peak memory if available, output size, output duration, and frame/audio sync.
3. Add a genuine app-level export integration fixture with generated short video + audio, overlays, and one transition. Verify exported frames, audible audio, duration, and repeat export after Undo/Redo. The existing worker smoke test is not full export-path coverage.

### P1 — tackle actual wall-clock bottlenecks
4. Replace full-file caption decode with source-range/streaming audio decode. Evaluate a maintained MP4 demuxer + WebCodecs path and compare it against FFmpeg extraction; preserve container support and Android fallback behavior.
5. Use the in-app stage report to profile Whisper model download, model initialization, decode, and inference separately. Benchmark WebGPU and WASM on supported devices and languages. Keep the compatibility path and do not make a model/version upgrade without comparing WER/quality, load time, steady-state time, cancellation behavior, and peak memory.
6. Profile cache hit rate and source staging. Tune the bounded FFmpeg FS budgets from device measurements; test many unique sources and alternating repeated clips to ensure the cache neither increases OOMs nor leaves stale virtual files.
7. Evaluate `@ffmpeg/core-mt` only after configuring cross-origin isolation for every web deployment and verifying Capacitor/WebView support. Multi-threading can improve throughput but increases CPU/memory pressure and is not a free upgrade.

### P1 — fault tolerance and UX
8. Add an export-cancel action with explicit semantics. FFmpeg WASM currently runs a long task in its worker; design termination/recovery so cancellation cleans workspace files and the next export reloads the engine safely.
9. Improve caption failures by classifying no audio, no speech, offline model download, GPU initialization/inference, and decode failure separately. Preserve the project unchanged on cancellation/failure.
10. Ensure export output and saved project are not reported complete until the output file has actually been saved/shared, and validate the second export after an engine recovery.

### P2 — editing foundation
11. Centralize edits as validated commands over candidate state; one edit equals one Undo step or no mutation.
12. Implement selected-lane ripple, all-unlocked-tracks ripple, slip, and roll as distinct commands. When a time cut crosses audio or video overlays, split source ranges correctly and preserve transforms/effects/source offsets. Never implement global ripple by indiscriminately shifting all `start` fields.
13. Add fractional frame-rate/VFR tests and separate project-frame positions from source timestamps.
14. Add keyboard shortcuts only after each shortcut routes through the same edit-command implementation.

## Merge and release policy

The trim/performance branch may merge after syntax, browser tests, FFmpeg worker integration, and Android APK build all pass on the same branch head. A merge to `main` should build and upload the APK artifact but must not automatically publish a GitHub Release. Release publication is opt-in through **Actions → Build APK → Run workflow → Publish a GitHub Release for this APK build**.

Before calling the latency work complete, run the representative on-device benchmarks above and record the actual timings. Do not publish a release based on a successful build alone.

BLOB SHA: d237eb8696dd341e9e108192f7af434049c361d5

# Speech captions: what needs the internet, what is stored where

Automatic captions use three separate pieces. They fail, cache and recover independently.

| Piece | Size | Where it comes from | Stored where | Needs internet? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Speech library (JavaScript) | 0.9 MB | **Bundled in the app** by the build (fetched from npm and SHA-256 verified by `scripts/fetch_speech_engine.sh`): `vendor/transformers/transformers.min.js` (@xenova/transformers 2.17.2) | Inside the APK | No |
| Speech runtime (WebAssembly) | 10 MB (+9 MB fallback) | **Bundled in the app**: `vendor/transformers/ort-wasm-simd.wasm`, `ort-wasm.wasm` | Inside the APK | No |
| Speech model (Whisper tiny) | ~40 MB | huggingface.co, first use only | Browser Cache Storage `transformers-cache` inside the app's private storage | **Yes, once.** Then no, while Android keeps the app's storage |

Optional GPU engine (transformers 3.x, `useWebGPU`): its library is loaded from cdn.jsdelivr.net each time it is used
(not bundled). If it cannot be loaded, MediaY uses the bundled CPU engine automatically.

## Behaviour
- **First captions run, online:** library + runtime load from the app, then the 40 MB model downloads with one overall percentage.
  Files that finished downloading are kept if the download is interrupted; the next attempt continues with the rest.
- **Later runs, offline:** work, provided the model files are still in the cache. Android can clear them if the user clears app
  storage or the phone is critically low on space. Then the next run needs the internet once more.
- **First run, offline:** MediaY says so plainly ("not downloaded yet… connect once, about 40 MB") instead of a technical error.
- **Damaged cache:** a model file that fails to parse is deleted once and downloaded again automatically.
- **Retries:** up to 3 model attempts (1.5 s, 3 s back-off). The online fallback copy of the library gets 3 attempts with a fresh URL
  each time (browsers remember a failed `import()` of the same URL).
- **Cancel:** checked between attempts and between 30-second audio windows. A download that is already in progress cannot be
  aborted mid-file (the library offers no way to), so the cancel takes effect when that step ends.

## Other downloads the app makes (not captions)
- FFmpeg core (31 MB, jsdelivr): downloaded on first export, cached in Cache Storage. **The first export needs the internet.**
- Fabric, Dexie, anime.js, Lucide and the DM Sans font load from CDNs on every start (the Android shell does not bundle them yet).

## Making captions fully offline later
Place the Whisper model files under `models/Xenova/whisper-tiny/` in the web bundle and set `env.allowLocalModels = true`,
`env.localModelPath = './models/'` in `createSpeechPipeline`. This is NOT done: the model could not be downloaded in the
build environment and it would add ~40 MB to the APK.

## Verification
`python tests/offline_speech_runtime.py` runs a real ONNX inference through the bundled library and runtime with every
external request blocked (it also fails if the bundled WASM is removed, which is the failure mode of a library-only bundle).

FILE: docs/timeline-trim-architecture.md
BLOB SHA: c2ad42c5f8d962dc2f30ddd582fd3d33502c396c

# MediaY Timeline Trimming: Architecture and Release Gates

## Current model and user-facing behavior

MediaY currently models the main video lane as a serial list. A clip's timeline duration is derived from `(out - in) / speed`; changing that duration shifts every later main-lane clip because the lane has no explicit gap items. Audio clips and overlays are separate timed items with absolute `start` values. Those independent lanes do not move automatically when the main lane shrinks.

The playhead trim controls intentionally operate on the selected item:

- **From here** keeps the selected item's content from the playhead onward.
- **To here** keeps its content up to the playhead.
- Main video maps timeline time to source time using clip speed.
- Audio trims source `in/out` and adjusts `start` when its lead-in is removed.
- Image/text overlays adjust timeline `start/dur`.
- Video overlays also carry `sourceIn` and `srcDur`; preview seeking, FFmpeg input seeking, and overlay-audio mixing must use the same source-time mapping.
- Trims require at least one project frame (currently 1/30 second) on both sides, pause playback before mutation, and remain undoable/redoable.

This is not yet global ripple editing. Shortening a main clip moves later main clips earlier, while audio and overlays retain their absolute times. The UI and documentation must not imply otherwise.

## Non-negotiable invariants

1. **No invalid ranges.** Each trim must leave a valid, positive source range and at least one project frame on either side. Do not mutate state when validation fails.
2. **One time mapping per media type.** Keep timeline time and source-media time separate. Playback, thumbnails, preview, audio, and export must agree on the same mapping.
3. **One edit, one history entry.** A user action either commits completely as one Undo step or leaves the project unchanged.
4. **Stop playback before structural edits.** Do not let the playback loop and an edit concurrently change the playhead or selected source frame.
5. **Never confuse viewport state with edit state.** Programmatic timeline scrolling must not trigger a user scrub. The expected scroll change is ignored once; genuine user scrolling continues to seek.
6. **Preserve source offsets.** When an overlay's timeline start changes, do not silently reset the source frame to zero. Reset `sourceIn` only when the underlying media is replaced.
7. **Keep the existing project shape readable.** Missing `sourceIn` in older saved projects means zero; normalization must not invalidate older projects.

## Next architecture: transactional edit commands

Before adding ripple, slip, or roll edits, centralize timeline changes behind one edit-command boundary. UI handlers should describe the intent; a shared edit layer should do the work.

Each command should:

1. Capture a stable target ID, lane, playhead frame, and edit mode. Resolve the ID again immediately before applying the edit.
2. Preflight finite times, media bounds, lane membership, clip duration, and all affected linked items.
3. Apply changes to a candidate project state, not the live state.
4. Validate the candidate state across every affected lane.
5. Commit the full state once, create one Undo entry, then refresh the timeline and preview from the committed state.
6. If media is missing, a range is invalid, or applying the command throws, discard the candidate state and keep the original edit untouched.

The existing snapshot-based Undo/Redo can remain as the first implementation, but it must only receive a fully validated committed state. The UI must never duplicate edit math across buttons, pointer drags, and keyboard shortcuts.

## Timebase and precision

The current timeline stores seconds as floating-point values and exports at 30 fps. One-frame trim boundaries are the correct immediate minimum, but the durable model should use integer project-frame positions for timeline edits and convert to source seconds at media boundaries. Centralize the project frame rate instead of scattering the number `30` across the application.

For variable-frame-rate media or source media at a different frame rate, timeline frame positions and source-media timestamps must remain distinct. Rounding should happen at well-defined boundaries, not after each edit. Tests should cover speeds below and above 1x, short clips, fractional source times, and clips near source EOF.

## Ripple scope must be explicit

Adopt separate commands rather than one ambiguous “trim” operation:

- **Trim item:** change only the selected item's source in/out or visible duration.
- **Ripple trim on the selected lane:** remove the selected-lane time and shift later items on that lane.
- **Ripple all tracks:** remove a project time range and shift other lanes according to an explicit policy.
- **Slip:** change source in/out together while preserving timeline position and duration.
- **Roll:** move a cut point between neighboring clips while preserving the combined duration.

Global ripple is not safe to implement by simply subtracting a duration from every later `start`. If the removed interval crosses an audio clip, split it into source ranges before and after the cut. If it crosses a video overlay, split it and preserve `sourceIn` on the right-hand fragment. Image/text overlays can be clipped by visible duration; audio/video items need source-range-aware splitting. Overlay transform, opacity, blend mode, keying, volume, and fade settings must survive both fragments. Provide separate controls for selected-lane ripple and all-track ripple so users can predict whether music and titles move.

## Regression and release gates

Every edit operation needs tests for:

- start/middle/end positions, one-frame fragments, zero/negative/non-finite times, and source EOF;
- normal speed, slow motion, fast motion, and a previously trimmed clip;
- video, audio, image, text, and video-overlay items;
- preview frame, exported video frame, and mixed audio agreeing on the same source time;
- Undo, Redo, autosave, project restore, and a failed edit leaving no partial mutation;
- programmatic auto-scroll, zoom, touch scrubbing, and playback not fighting over the playhead;
- existing export-fade and Adjust/Blending smoke tests remaining green.

Do not merge a trim/ripple change until JavaScript syntax, browser smoke tests, real FFmpeg worker integration, and Android build checks pass. Then verify on a real Android device with a saved project, at least one speed-adjusted clip, one video overlay with audio, and a second export after Undo/Redo. A green browser test alone does not prove the device export path.

## Open-source behavior references

Use these as behavior references, not as code to paste into MediaY:

- Kdenlive documents distinct normal/overwrite/insert modes, plus ripple trim to playhead: https://docs.kdenlive.org/en/user_interface/menu/tool_menu.html and https://docs.kdenlive.org/en/user_interface/editing.html
- Shotcut distinguishes normal trim from ripple trim and includes explicit “Ripple All Tracks” and “Ripple Markers” settings: https://www.shotcut.org/howtos/keyboard-shortcuts/ and https://www.shotcut.org/notes/configuration/

The product advantage should be predictability: frame-accurate, reversible edits with identical preview/export timing and an explicit answer to “which tracks will move?” before destructive timeline operations.

FILE: ffmpeg-worker-smoke.html
BLOB SHA: 8aed7f17b54ea7eb6dc359bdd68ea5b2c2708cd0

<!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
  <title>MediaY FFmpeg Worker Smoke Test</title>
  <script src="./vendor/ffmpeg/ffmpeg.js"></script>
</head>
<body>
  <h1>MediaY FFmpeg worker integration test</h1>
  <p id="stage">Starting…</p>
  <pre id="log"></pre>
  <script>
  (async () => {
    const stage = document.querySelector('#stage');
    const log = document.querySelector('#log');
    const write = (message) => { stage.textContent = message; log.textContent += message + '\n'; };
    let ff;
    try {
      if (!window.FFmpegWASM || !FFmpegWASM.FFmpeg) throw new Error('Local FFmpeg wrapper did not expose FFmpegWASM.FFmpeg.');
      const base = 'https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@ffmpeg/core@0.12.10/dist/umd/';
      const objectUrls = [];
      const remoteBlobURL = async (url, mime) => {
        write('Downloading ' + url.split('/').pop() + '…');
        const response = await fetch(url, { mode: 'cors', cache: 'force-cache' });
        if (!response.ok) throw new Error('HTTP ' + response.status + ' loading ' + url);
        const objectURL = URL.createObjectURL(new Blob([await response.blob()], {type:mime}));
        objectUrls.push(objectURL);
        return objectURL;
      };
      ff = new FFmpegWASM.FFmpeg();
      ff.on('log', ({message}) => { if (/error|failed|invalid/i.test(message || '')) log.textContent += String(message) + '\n'; });
      const coreURL = await remoteBlobURL(base + 'ffmpeg-core.js', 'text/javascript');
      const wasmURL = await remoteBlobURL(base + 'ffmpeg-core.wasm', 'application/wasm');
      write('Starting local same-origin worker…');
      await ff.load({coreURL, wasmURL});
      if (!ff.loaded) throw new Error('FFmpeg load resolved but the worker is not marked loaded.');
      write('Rendering a small test MP4…');
      const code = await ff.exec(['-f','lavfi','-i','color=c=red:s=64x64:r=10:d=0.5','-frames:v','5','-c:v','libx264','-pix_fmt','yuv420p','smoke.mp4']);
      if (code !== 0) throw new Error('FFmpeg exited with code ' + code);
      const output = await ff.readFile('smoke.mp4');
      if (!output || output.length < 100) throw new Error('FFmpeg returned an empty MP4.');

      // Regression test for the exact phone failure: shorthand d=.4 is rejected by this core.
      write('Testing fade and Adjust filter chain…');
      const filters = "fps=10,eq=brightness=0.12:contrast=1.2:saturation=1.35,curves=all='0/0 0.25/0.28 0.5/0.5 0.75/0.78 1/1',unsharp=5:5:1.0:5:5:0.0,noise=alls=5:allf=t+u,fade=type=in:duration=0.2,fade=type=out:start_time=0.3:duration=0.2";
      const effectCode = await ff.exec(['-f','lavfi','-i','color=c=red:s=64x64:r=10:d=0.5','-vf',filters,'-frames:v','5','-c:v','libx264','-pix_fmt','yuv420p','effects-smoke.mp4']);
      if (effectCode !== 0) throw new Error('FFmpeg could not encode fade + Adjust filter chain (code ' + effectCode + ').');
      const effects = await ff.readFile('effects-smoke.mp4');
      if (!effects || effects.length < 100) throw new Error('FFmpeg returned an empty adjusted/faded MP4.');

      write('Testing alpha-masked blend mode…');
      const canvas = document.createElement('canvas');
      canvas.width = 64; canvas.height = 64;
      const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
      ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(0,255,0,0.5)'; ctx.fillRect(16,16,32,32);
      const png = await new Promise(resolve => canvas.toBlob(resolve,'image/png'));
      await ff.writeFile('blend-overlay.png', new Uint8Array(await png.arrayBuffer()));
      const blendGraph = "[0:v]format=yuv444p,split[baseKeep][baseBlend];[1:v]scale=64:64,format=rgba,split[overlayColor][overlayAlpha];[overlayColor]format=yuv444p[overlayYuv];[baseBlend][overlayYuv]blend=all_mode=multiply:all_opacity=1[blendFrame];[overlayAlpha]alphaextract,format=yuv444p,geq=lum='(lum(X,Y)-16)*255/219':cb='(lum(X,Y)-16)*255/219':cr='(lum(X,Y)-16)*255/219'[blendMask];[baseKeep][blendFrame][blendMask]maskedmerge=enable='between(t,0,0.5)'[out]";
      const blendCode = await ff.exec(['-f','lavfi','-i','color=c=red:s=64x64:r=10:d=0.5','-i','blend-overlay.png','-filter_complex',blendGraph,'-map','[out]','-c:v','libx264','-pix_fmt','yuv420p','-t','0.5','blend-smoke.mp4']);
      if (blendCode !== 0) throw new Error('FFmpeg could not render the masked overlay blend mode (code ' + blendCode + ').');
      const blendOut = await ff.readFile('blend-smoke.mp4');
      if (!blendOut || blendOut.length < 100) throw new Error('FFmpeg returned an empty blended MP4.');
      window.testResult = {ok:true, bytes:output.length, adjustedBytes:effects.length, blendedBytes:blendOut.length, message:'same-origin worker encoded MP4, fade/Adjust effects, and alpha-masked blend'};
      write('PASS — encoded base, fade/Adjust, and blend test videos.');
      ff.terminate();
      objectUrls.forEach(URL.revokeObjectURL);
    } catch (error) {
      window.testResult = {ok:false, error:String(error && error.message || error), stack:String(error && error.stack || '')};
      write('FAIL — ' + window.testResult.error);
      try { if (ff) ff.terminate(); } catch (_) {}
    }
  })();
  </script>
</body>
</html>

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Generate MediaY launcher icons from the selected Neon Aperture Y mark."""
import glob
import os
import re
import sys
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw

BACKGROUND = (8, 13, 24, 255)
CYAN = (0, 231, 242, 255)
BLUE = (22, 139, 255, 255)
VIOLET = (139, 92, 246, 255)
WHITE = (243, 247, 255, 255)


def render_icon(size: int, maskable: bool = False) -> Image.Image:
    scale = 4
    side = size * scale
    image = Image.new("RGBA", (side, side), (0, 0, 0, 0))
    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(image, "RGBA")
    pad = 0 if maskable else int(side * .035)
    radius = 0 if maskable else int(side * .22)
    draw.rounded_rectangle((pad, pad, side-pad-1, side-pad-1), radius=radius, fill=BACKGROUND)
    if not maskable:
        draw.rounded_rectangle((pad+2, pad+2, side-pad-3, side-pad-3),
                               radius=max(1, radius-2), outline=CYAN, width=max(2, side//180))
    # Leave safe space for Android's adaptive-icon mask.
    factor = .78 if maskable else .88
    def p(x, y):
        x = 64 + (x-64)*factor
        y = 64 + (y-64)*factor
        return (int(x/128*side), int(y/128*side))
    def poly(points, fill, outline=None):
        draw.polygon([p(x,y) for x,y in points], fill=fill, outline=outline)
    # Y arms: cyan left and porcelain-white right.
    poly([(13,12),(36,12),(58,49),(48,62),(27,38)], CYAN)
    poly([(115,12),(92,12),(70,49),(80,62),(101,38)], WHITE)
    # Segmented cuts on the left arm.
    poly([(20,25),(38,25),(42,32),(24,32)], BACKGROUND)
    poly([(28,39),(46,39),(50,46),(32,46)], BACKGROUND)
    # Circular camera aperture with violet ring and cyan inner edge.
    cx, cy = p(64,52)
    rad = int(26/128*side*factor)
    draw.ellipse((cx-rad,cy-rad,cx+rad,cy+rad), fill=(15,18,43,255), outline=VIOLET, width=max(3,side//42))
    inner = int(20/128*side*factor)
    draw.ellipse((cx-inner,cy-inner,cx+inner,cy+inner), outline=CYAN, width=max(2,side//100))
    # Six shutter blades, all meeting around a small dark iris.
    poly([(64,32),(79,40),(75,54),(62,56),(52,43)], CYAN)
    poly([(79,40),(85,53),(75,66),(65,55),(75,54)], VIOLET)
    poly([(85,53),(78,68),(63,72),(64,57),(75,66)], (105,65,210,255))
    poly([(78,68),(63,72),(50,63),(62,54),(63,72)], BLUE)
    poly([(50,63),(44,50),(52,37),(62,49),(62,54)], CYAN)
    poly([(44,50),(52,37),(64,32),(62,49),(52,54)], (47,124,245,255))
    iris = int(5/128*side*factor)
    draw.ellipse((cx-iris,cy-iris,cx+iris,cy+iris), fill=BACKGROUND)
    # Stem and restrained timeline tracks.
    poly([(49,73),(64,79),(79,73),(75,120),(53,120)], BLUE)
    draw.line([p(64,80),p(64,119)], fill=WHITE, width=max(2,side//150))
    poly([(8,92),(42,92),(42,99),(8,99)], CYAN)
    poly([(8,105),(42,105),(42,109),(8,109)], BLUE)
    poly([(86,92),(120,92),(120,99),(86,99)], VIOLET)
    poly([(86,105),(120,105),(120,109),(86,109)], (105,65,210,255))
    return image.resize((size,size), Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)


def create():
    render_icon(192).save("icon-192.png", "PNG", optimize=True)
    render_icon(512).save("icon-512.png", "PNG", optimize=True)
    render_icon(512, maskable=True).save("icon-maskable-512.png", "PNG", optimize=True)
    print("Generated selected MediaY Neon Aperture icons.")


def apply():
    resources = "android/app/src/main/res"
    for path in glob.glob(resources + "/mipmap-*/ic_launcher*.png"):
        foreground = "foreground" in os.path.basename(path)
        source = "icon-maskable-512.png" if foreground else "icon-512.png"
        with Image.open(source) as icon:
            size = Image.open(path).size
            icon.convert("RGBA").resize(size, Image.Resampling.LANCZOS).save(path)
    for path in glob.glob(resources + "/drawable*/splash.png"):
        with Image.open(path) as old:
            width, height = old.size
        image = Image.new("RGBA", (width, height), BACKGROUND)
        side = int(min(width, height) * .4)
        with Image.open("icon-512.png") as icon:
            logo = icon.convert("RGBA").resize((side, side), Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)
        image.paste(logo, ((width-side)//2,(height-side)//2), logo)
        image.save(path)
    background_xml = resources + "/values/ic_launcher_background.xml"
    if os.path.exists(background_xml):
        with open(background_xml, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            source = f.read()
        with open(background_xml, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(re.sub(r"#[0-9a-fA-F]{6}", "#080D18", source, count=1))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    mode = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "before"
    create() if mode == "before" else apply()

FILE: mediay-core.js
BLOB SHA: 67b493ee21203420d7464793e7b235f2a69a7dac

/* MediaY core logic — pure functions with no DOM, no network and no global state.
   Loaded as a browser global (window.MediaYCore) and as a Node module (for tests).
   Everything here is deterministic so it can be verified automatically. */
(function (root, factory) {
  if (typeof module === 'object' && module.exports) module.exports = factory();
  else root.MediaYCore = factory();
}(typeof self !== 'undefined' ? self : this, function () {
  'use strict';

  const TIMELINE_FPS = 30;
  const MIN_FRAME = 1 / TIMELINE_FPS;
  const num = (v, d = 0) => (Number.isFinite(Number(v)) ? Number(v) : d);
  const clamp = (v, lo, hi) => Math.max(lo, Math.min(hi, v));

  /* ------------------------------------------------------------------
     1. Speech activity detection (energy based, adaptive, no model)
     ------------------------------------------------------------------ */
  const ACTIVITY = Object.freeze({
    hopSeconds: 0.02,        // 20 ms analysis frames
    closeGapSeconds: 0.15,   // bridge short pauses inside a phrase
    minRunSeconds: 0.06,     // ignore clicks/pops shorter than this
    minDynamicRangeDb: 14,   // below this the signal has no usable speech/noise contrast
    maxVoicedRatio: 0.98     // above this it is music/noise everywhere: do not trust it
  });

  function percentile(sortedAscending, q) {
    if (!sortedAscending.length) return 0;
    const i = clamp(Math.round(q * (sortedAscending.length - 1)), 0, sortedAscending.length - 1);
    return sortedAscending[i];
  }

  /** Returns { hop, frames, voiced:Uint8Array, intervals:[[s,e]...], reliable, thresholdDb, duration } */
  function analyzeSpeechActivity(pcm, sampleRate = 16000) {
    const hopSamples = Math.max(1, Math.round(sampleRate * ACTIVITY.hopSeconds));
    const frames = Math.floor((pcm ? pcm.length : 0) / hopSamples);
    const duration = pcm ? pcm.length / sampleRate : 0;
    const result = { hop: ACTIVITY.hopSeconds, frames, voiced: new Uint8Array(frames), intervals: [], reliable: false, thresholdDb: -60, duration, voicedRatio: 0 };
    if (frames < 5) return result;

    const db = new Float32Array(frames);
    for (let f = 0; f < frames; f++) {
      let sum = 0;
      const from = f * hopSamples;
      for (let i = from; i < from + hopSamples; i++) sum += pcm[i] * pcm[i];
      db[f] = 20 * Math.log10(Math.sqrt(sum / hopSamples) + 1e-9);
    }
    const sorted = Array.from(db).sort((a, b) => a - b);
    const low = percentile(sorted, 0.05), high = percentile(sorted, 0.95);
    result.dynamicRangeDb = high - low;
    // Threshold: above the noise floor, but always well below the loud speech level.
    const threshold = Math.min(Math.max(low + 10, -55), high - 18);
    result.thresholdDb = threshold;
    if (high < -50) return result; // essentially silent

    const raw = new Uint8Array(frames);
    for (let f = 0; f < frames; f++) raw[f] = db[f] > threshold ? 1 : 0;

    // 1) close short gaps, 2) drop short runs
    const closeFrames = Math.round(ACTIVITY.closeGapSeconds / ACTIVITY.hopSeconds);
    const minRun = Math.max(1, Math.round(ACTIVITY.minRunSeconds / ACTIVITY.hopSeconds));
    const runs = [];
    let start = -1;
    for (let f = 0; f <= frames; f++) {
      const on = f < frames && raw[f] === 1;
      if (on && start < 0) start = f;
      if (!on && start >= 0) { runs.push([start, f]); start = -1; }
    }
    const merged = [];
    for (const run of runs) {
      const last = merged[merged.length - 1];
      if (last && run[0] - last[1] <= closeFrames) last[1] = run[1];
      else merged.push([run[0], run[1]]);
    }
    let voicedFrames = 0;
    for (const [a, b] of merged) {
      if (b - a < minRun) continue;
      result.intervals.push([a * ACTIVITY.hopSeconds, b * ACTIVITY.hopSeconds]);
      for (let f = a; f < b; f++) result.voiced[f] = 1;
      voicedFrames += b - a;
    }
    result.voicedRatio = voicedFrames / frames;
    result.reliable = result.dynamicRangeDb >= ACTIVITY.minDynamicRangeDb &&
      result.voicedRatio <= ACTIVITY.maxVoicedRatio && result.intervals.length > 0;
    return result;
  }

  /* ------------------------------------------------------------------
     2. Silence-aware chunk planning for Whisper (max 30 s windows)
     ------------------------------------------------------------------ */
  /** Splits [0,total) into windows <= maxSeconds, preferring to cut in the middle of a pause. */
  function planCaptionChunks(totalSeconds, activity, opts = {}) {
    const maxS = opts.maxSeconds || 30, searchS = opts.searchSeconds || 8, minS = opts.minSeconds || 12;
    const plan = [];
    let start = 0;
    while (totalSeconds - start > maxS + 1e-6) {
      let cut = start + maxS;
      if (activity && activity.reliable) {
        // Look for the longest pause (unvoiced run) in [start+maxS-searchS, start+maxS].
        const lo = Math.max(start + minS, start + maxS - searchS), hi = start + maxS;
        let best = null;
        let gapStart = null;
        const f0 = Math.floor(lo / activity.hop), f1 = Math.min(activity.frames, Math.floor(hi / activity.hop));
        for (let f = f0; f <= f1; f++) {
          const silent = f < f1 && !activity.voiced[f];
          if (silent && gapStart === null) gapStart = f;
          if (!silent && gapStart !== null) {
            const len = f - gapStart;
            if (len >= 5 && (!best || len > best.len)) best = { from: gapStart, len };
            gapStart = null;
          }
        }
        if (best) cut = (best.from + best.len / 2) * activity.hop;
      }
      plan.push([start, cut]);
      start = cut;
    }
    if (totalSeconds - start > 1e-6) plan.push([start, totalSeconds]);
    return plan;
  }

  /**
   * Builds bounded decode windows with overlap while assigning each source-time position
   * to exactly one window. This gives Whisper boundary context without duplicate captions.
   */
  function planCaptionStreamWindows(totalSeconds, windowSeconds = 600, overlapSeconds = 1.5) {
    const total = Number(totalSeconds), width = Number(windowSeconds);
    if (!Number.isFinite(total) || total <= 0 || !Number.isFinite(width) || width <= 0) return [];
    const overlap = Math.max(0, Math.min(Number(overlapSeconds) || 0, width / 2));
    const half = overlap / 2, count = Math.ceil(total / width), windows = [];
    for (let index = 0; index < count; index++) {
      const coreStartSeconds = index * width;
      const coreEndSeconds = Math.min(total, coreStartSeconds + width);
      const offsetSeconds = Math.max(0, coreStartSeconds - half);
      const decodeEndSeconds = Math.min(total, coreEndSeconds + half);
      windows.push({
        index, count, offsetSeconds,
        durationSeconds: decodeEndSeconds - offsetSeconds,
        coreStartSeconds, coreEndSeconds,
        ownershipStartSeconds: index === 0 ? 0 : coreStartSeconds - half,
        ownershipEndSeconds: index === count - 1 ? total : coreEndSeconds - half
      });
    }
    return windows;
  }

  /* ------------------------------------------------------------------
     3. Caption alignment: ASR segments + speech activity -> timed lines
     ------------------------------------------------------------------ */
  const CAPTION = Object.freeze({
    maxWords: 4, maxChars: 30, pauseBreakSeconds: 0.55,
    startSlackBefore: 0.9, endSlackAfter: 0.9,
    lead: 0.04, hold: 0.14, minDuration: 0.45, bridgeGap: 0.3, wordTrust: 0.5
  });

  const wordWeight = w => Math.max(1, w.replace(/[^\p{L}\p{N}]/gu, '').length) + 0.6;

  /** Collapse pathological Whisper token-repetition loops (see FilmCraft crates/speech/src/whisper.rs `repeating`). */
  function stripRepeatingLoops(text) {
    const tokens = String(text || '').trim().split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean);
    const norm = w => w.toLowerCase().replace(/[^\p{L}\p{N}]/gu, '');
    const out = [];
    let i = 0;
    while (i < tokens.length) {
      let collapsed = false;
      for (let n = 1; n <= 6; n++) {
        // Conservative thresholds: preserve natural emphasis and laughter.
        const minReps = n === 1 ? 8 : 5;
        const minTokens = 8;
        if (i + n * minReps > tokens.length || n * minReps < minTokens) continue;
        const pat = tokens.slice(i, i + n).map(norm);
        if (pat.some(p => !p)) continue;
        let reps = 1;
        while (i + (reps + 1) * n <= tokens.length) {
          let match = true;
          for (let k = 0; k < n; k++) {
            if (norm(tokens[i + reps * n + k]) !== pat[k]) { match = false; break; }
          }
          if (!match) break;
          reps++;
        }
        if (reps >= minReps) {
          out.push(...tokens.slice(i, i + n));
          i += reps * n;
          collapsed = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (!collapsed) out.push(tokens[i++]);
    }
    return out.join(' ');
  }

  // Remove only rapid, long runs of identical short Whisper segments. Repeated
  // phrases separated by real pauses are legitimate speech and must survive.
  function dropIdenticalSegmentRuns(segments, maxGapSeconds = 1.5, minRun = 8) {
    const key = text => String(text || '').toLowerCase().replace(/[^\p{L}\p{N}]+/gu, ' ').trim();
    const out = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < segments.length;) {
      const currentKey = key(segments[i].text);
      let j = i + 1;
      while (currentKey && j < segments.length && key(segments[j].text) === currentKey) {
        const prevEnd = Number(segments[j - 1].end);
        const nextStart = Number(segments[j].start);
        if (!Number.isFinite(prevEnd) || !Number.isFinite(nextStart) || nextStart - prevEnd > maxGapSeconds) break;
        j++;
      }
      const short = String(segments[i].text || '').trim().split(/\s+/).length <= 8;
      if (currentKey && short && j - i >= minRun) out.push(segments[i]);
      else out.push(...segments.slice(i, j));
      i = j;
    }
    return out;
  }

  function voicedWithin(activity, a, b) {
    const out = [];
    for (const [s, e] of activity.intervals) {
      if (e <= a) continue;
      if (s >= b) break;
      out.push([Math.max(s, a), Math.min(e, b)]);
    }
    return out;
  }

  function saneWordTimes(list) {
    let previous = -Infinity;
    for (const w of list) {
      const a = Number(w.start), b = Number(w.end);
      if (!Number.isFinite(a) || !Number.isFinite(b) || b < a || a < previous - 0.05 || b - a > 4) return false;
      previous = a;
    }
    return true;
  }

  /** Joins model word-level output ([{text,start,end}]) into phrase segments at pauses and sentence ends. */
  function groupWordsIntoPhrases(wordList, opts = {}) {
    const gap = opts.gapSeconds || 0.35, maxWords = opts.maxWords || 14;
    const phrases = [];
    let cur = null;
    const cleaned = (wordList || []).map(w => ({ text: String(w.text || '').trim(), start: num(w.start), end: Number.isFinite(Number(w.end)) ? Number(w.end) : NaN }))
      .filter(w => w.text);
    cleaned.forEach((w, i) => {
      if (!Number.isFinite(w.end)) w.end = Math.max(w.start + 0.2, cleaned[i + 1] ? cleaned[i + 1].start : w.start + 0.4);
      if (/^[,.;:!?%…]+$/.test(w.text) && cur) { // stray punctuation token joins the previous word
        cur.words[cur.words.length - 1].text += w.text; cur.words[cur.words.length - 1].end = Math.max(cur.words[cur.words.length - 1].end, w.end);
        cur.text += w.text; cur.end = Math.max(cur.end, w.end); return;
      }
      if (cur && (w.start - cur.end > gap || cur.words.length >= maxWords || /[.!?…]["')\]]?$/.test(cur.words[cur.words.length - 1].text))) { phrases.push(cur); cur = null; }
      if (!cur) cur = { text: '', start: w.start, end: w.end, words: [] };
      cur.words.push({ text: w.text, start: w.start, end: w.end });
      cur.text = cur.words.map(x => x.text).join(' ');
      cur.end = Math.max(cur.end, w.end);
    });
    if (cur) phrases.push(cur);
    return phrases;
  }

  /** True when at least `minSeconds` of detected speech lies inside [a,b]. */
  function hasSpeech(activity, a, b, minSeconds = 0.25) {
    if (!activity || !activity.reliable) return true; // unknown: let the speech model decide
    let total = 0;
    for (const [s, e] of voicedWithin(activity, a, b)) total += e - s;
    return total >= minSeconds;
  }

  /** Map a position along the voiced axis (0..total) back to real time. */
  function voicedToTime(intervals, position) {
    let remaining = position;
    for (const [s, e] of intervals) {
      const len = e - s;
      if (remaining <= len) return s + remaining;
      remaining -= len;
    }
    return intervals.length ? intervals[intervals.length - 1][1] : 0;
  }

  /** Merge mixed Whisper output without moving phrase segments across neighbouring word runs. */
  function mergeCaptionSegments(entries) {
    const ordered = (entries || []).filter(entry => entry && String(entry.text || '').trim())
      .map(entry => ({ ...entry, text: String(entry.text).trim() }));
    const output = [];
    let wordRun = [];
    const flushWords = () => {
      if (wordRun.length) output.push(...groupWordsIntoPhrases(wordRun));
      wordRun = [];
    };
    for (const entry of ordered) {
      if (entry.word) wordRun.push(entry);
      else { flushWords(); output.push({ text: entry.text, start: num(entry.start), end: Number.isFinite(Number(entry.end)) ? Number(entry.end) : NaN }); }
    }
    flushWords();
    return output;
  }

  /**
   * segments: [{ text, start, end }] in seconds relative to the same origin as `activity`.
   * Returns lines: [{ text, start, end }] — sorted, non-overlapping, inside [0,duration].
   */
  function buildCaptionLines(segments, activity, duration, opts = {}) {
    const cfg = { ...CAPTION, ...opts };
    const usable = activity && activity.reliable;
    const clean = (segments || [])
      .map(seg => ({
        text: stripRepeatingLoops(String(seg && seg.text || '').trim()),
        start: num(seg && seg.start, 0),
        end: seg && Number.isFinite(Number(seg.end)) ? Number(seg.end) : NaN,
        words: Array.isArray(seg && seg.words) ? seg.words : null
      }))
      .filter(seg => seg.text && !/^[\[(♪♫].*[\])♪♫]$/.test(seg.text) && !/^[♪♫\s]+$/.test(seg.text))
      .sort((a, b) => a.start - b.start);
    const deduplicated = dropIdenticalSegmentRuns(clean);

    const words = []; // { text, start, end, segmentIndex }
    let previousEnd = 0;
    deduplicated.forEach((seg, index) => {
      const tokens = seg.text.split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean);
      if (!tokens.length) return;
      let s = clamp(seg.start, 0, duration);
      let e = Number.isFinite(seg.end) ? clamp(seg.end, s, duration) : Math.min(duration, s + tokens.length * 0.35);
      if (e - s < 0.05) e = Math.min(duration, s + Math.max(0.2, tokens.length * 0.25));
      const next = deduplicated[index + 1];
      let rs = s, re = e, voiced = null;
      if (usable) {
        const windowStart = Math.max(previousEnd, s - cfg.startSlackBefore);
        const nextStart = next ? clamp(next.start, e, duration) : duration;
        const windowEnd = Math.min(duration, Math.min(e + cfg.endSlackAfter, Math.max(e, (e + nextStart) / 2 + 0.15)));
        const inside = voicedWithin(activity, windowStart, Math.max(windowStart, windowEnd));
        if (inside.length) {
          // The segment must actually overlap speech, otherwise keep the model's own times.
          const overlaps = inside.some(([a, b]) => b > s - 0.2 && a < e + 0.2);
          if (overlaps) {
            const used = inside.filter(([a, b]) => b > s - cfg.startSlackBefore && a < e + cfg.endSlackAfter);
            // Tighten against pauses (FilmCraft vad.rs `split_at_pauses` / `trim_edges`):
            // 1) A tiny click/scrap before a >= 200ms pause prior to `s` is not the start of this segment.
            while (used.length > 1 && used[0][1] <= s + 0.05 && used[1][0] - used[0][1] >= 0.2 && used[0][1] - used[0][0] < 0.12) {
              used.shift();
            }
            // 2) When Whisper's contiguous segment end spills across a >= 200ms pause into the next segment's
            //    speech onset, end this segment at the pause so `previousEnd` does not push `next` late.
            if (next && used.length > 1) {
              const minExpectedSpeech = Math.min(0.25, tokens.length * 0.08);
              while (used.length > 1) {
                const lastInt = used[used.length - 1];
                const prevInt = used[used.length - 2];
                const pauseGap = lastInt[0] - prevInt[1];
                const speechBefore = used.slice(0, -1).reduce((acc, [a, b]) => acc + (b - a), 0);
                const rawContinuesIntoNext = activity.intervals.some(([ia, ib]) => ia <= lastInt[0] + 1e-4 && ib >= lastInt[1] - 1e-4 && ib > next.start + 0.05);
                if (pauseGap >= 0.2 && speechBefore >= minExpectedSpeech && (rawContinuesIntoNext || lastInt[0] >= Math.min(e, nextStart) - cfg.startSlackBefore)) {
                  used.pop();
                } else {
                  break;
                }
              }
            }
            if (used.length) {
              rs = used[0][0];
              re = used[used.length - 1][1];
              voiced = voicedWithin(activity, rs, re);
            }
          }
        }
      }
      if (re - rs < 0.05) { rs = s; re = e; voiced = null; }
      previousEnd = re;

      // Speech-weighted positions: words share the detected speech time in proportion to their length.
      const weights = tokens.map(wordWeight);
      const totalWeight = weights.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
      const axis = voiced && voiced.length ? voiced : [[rs, re]];
      const axisLength = axis.reduce((a, [x, y]) => a + (y - x), 0);
      // Model word times (when supplied and sane) keep their relative spacing, fitted to the same start/end.
      const modelWords = seg.words && seg.words.length === tokens.length && saneWordTimes(seg.words) ? seg.words : null;
      const trust = modelWords ? clamp(num(cfg.wordTrust, 0.5), 0, 1) : 0;
      const modelFrom = modelWords ? num(modelWords[0].start) : 0;
      const modelSpan = modelWords ? Math.max(1e-3, num(modelWords[modelWords.length - 1].end) - modelFrom) : 1;
      let acc = 0;
      tokens.forEach((token, i) => {
        const from = acc / totalWeight * axisLength;
        acc += weights[i];
        const to = acc / totalWeight * axisLength;
        let ws = voicedToTime(axis, from), we = voicedToTime(axis, to);
        let speechInterval = 0;
        if (voiced && voiced.length) {
          let remaining = (from + to) / 2;
          for (let intervalIndex = 0; intervalIndex < axis.length; intervalIndex++) {
            const intervalLength = axis[intervalIndex][1] - axis[intervalIndex][0];
            if (remaining <= intervalLength || intervalIndex === axis.length - 1) {
              speechInterval = intervalIndex;
              const [intervalStart, intervalEnd] = axis[intervalIndex];
              ws = clamp(ws, intervalStart, intervalEnd);
              we = clamp(we, intervalStart, intervalEnd);
              if (we - ws < 0.04) {
                const center = clamp((ws + we) / 2, intervalStart, intervalEnd);
                ws = Math.max(intervalStart, center - 0.02);
                we = Math.min(intervalEnd, Math.max(ws + 0.04, center + 0.02));
              }
              break;
            }
            remaining -= intervalLength;
          }
        }
        if (modelWords) {
          const ms = rs + (num(modelWords[i].start) - modelFrom) / modelSpan * (re - rs);
          const me = rs + (num(modelWords[i].end) - modelFrom) / modelSpan * (re - rs);
          ws = trust * ms + (1 - trust) * ws; we = trust * me + (1 - trust) * we;
        }
        words.push({ text: token, start: ws, end: Math.max(we, ws + 0.04), segmentIndex: index, speechInterval });
      });
    });

    // Group words into display lines. Break on pauses, on the word/char limit, and at segment ends.
    const lines = [];
    let current = [];
    const flush = () => {
      if (!current.length) return;
      lines.push({ text: current.map(w => w.text).join(' '), start: current[0].start, end: current[current.length - 1].end });
      current = [];
    };
    words.forEach((word, i) => {
      const prev = current[current.length - 1];
      const chars = current.reduce((a, w) => a + w.text.length + 1, 0) + word.text.length;
      if (prev && (word.segmentIndex !== prev.segmentIndex || word.speechInterval !== prev.speechInterval || word.start - prev.end > cfg.pauseBreakSeconds || current.length >= cfg.maxWords || chars > cfg.maxChars)) flush();
      current.push(word);
      if (/[.!?…]$/.test(word.text) && words[i + 1]) flush();
    });
    flush();

    // Final timing pass: lead-in, hold, no overlaps, no flicker between near-adjacent lines.
    lines.forEach(line => {
      line.start = Math.max(0, line.start - cfg.lead);
      line.end = line.end + cfg.hold;
    });
    for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
      const line = lines[i], next = lines[i + 1];
      if (next) {
        if (next.start - line.end < cfg.bridgeGap) line.end = Math.max(line.start + MIN_FRAME, next.start);
        if (line.end > next.start) line.end = next.start;
      }
      if (line.end - line.start < cfg.minDuration) {
        line.end = Math.min(next ? next.start : duration, line.start + cfg.minDuration);
      }
      line.end = Math.min(line.end, duration);
      if (line.end - line.start < MIN_FRAME) line.end = Math.min(duration, line.start + MIN_FRAME);
    }
    return lines.filter(l => l.end - l.start >= MIN_FRAME * 0.99 && l.start < duration);
  }

  const resampleTableCache = new Map();
  /** Windowed-sinc resampling with a cached polyphase table to suppress aliasing without per-sample trig overhead. */
  function resamplePcm(pcm, inputRate, outputRate) {
    if (!pcm || !pcm.length || !(inputRate > 0) || !(outputRate > 0)) return new Float32Array(0);
    if (inputRate === outputRate) return Float32Array.from(pcm);
    const ratio = inputRate / outputRate;
    const outputLength = Math.max(1, Math.round(pcm.length / ratio));
    const output = new Float32Array(outputLength);
    const radius = 12;
    const taps = radius * 2;
    const isIntRatio = Math.abs(ratio - Math.round(ratio)) < 1e-9;
    const PHASES = isIntRatio ? 1 : 512;
    const cacheKey = `${inputRate}:${outputRate}`;
    let table = resampleTableCache.get(cacheKey);
    if (!table) {
      const cutoff = Math.min(0.5, 0.5 / ratio) * 0.92;
      const sinc = x => Math.abs(x) < 1e-8 ? 1 : Math.sin(Math.PI * x) / (Math.PI * x);
      table = new Float32Array(PHASES * taps);
      for (let p = 0; p < PHASES; p++) {
        const frac = p / PHASES;
        const base = p * taps;
        let weightSum = 0;
        for (let k = 0; k < taps; k++) {
          const offset = k - radius + 1;
          const distance = offset - frac;
          const normalized = distance / radius;
          if (Math.abs(normalized) >= 1) continue;
          const win = 0.5 + 0.5 * Math.cos(Math.PI * normalized);
          const w = 2 * cutoff * sinc(2 * cutoff * distance) * win;
          table[base + k] = w;
          weightSum += w;
        }
        if (weightSum > 0) {
          const inv = 1 / weightSum;
          for (let k = 0; k < taps; k++) table[base + k] *= inv;
        }
      }
      resampleTableCache.set(cacheKey, table);
    }

    const pcmLen = pcm.length;
    const safeStart = Math.ceil(radius / ratio);
    const safeEnd = Math.min(outputLength, Math.floor((pcmLen - radius - 1) / ratio));
    for (let i = 0; i < outputLength; i++) {
      const position = i * ratio;
      const center = position | 0;
      const firstSample = center - radius + 1;
      if (i >= safeStart && i < safeEnd) {
        const base = isIntRatio ? 0 : Math.min(PHASES - 1, ((position - center) * PHASES + 0.5) | 0) * taps;
        output[i] =
          pcm[firstSample] * table[base] + pcm[firstSample + 1] * table[base + 1] +
          pcm[firstSample + 2] * table[base + 2] + pcm[firstSample + 3] * table[base + 3] +
          pcm[firstSample + 4] * table[base + 4] + pcm[firstSample + 5] * table[base + 5] +
          pcm[firstSample + 6] * table[base + 6] + pcm[firstSample + 7] * table[base + 7] +
          pcm[firstSample + 8] * table[base + 8] + pcm[firstSample + 9] * table[base + 9] +
          pcm[firstSample + 10] * table[base + 10] + pcm[firstSample + 11] * table[base + 11] +
          pcm[firstSample + 12] * table[base + 12] + pcm[firstSample + 13] * table[base + 13] +
          pcm[firstSample + 14] * table[base + 14] + pcm[firstSample + 15] * table[base + 15] +
          pcm[firstSample + 16] * table[base + 16] + pcm[firstSample + 17] * table[base + 17] +
          pcm[firstSample + 18] * table[base + 18] + pcm[firstSample + 19] * table[base + 19] +
          pcm[firstSample + 20] * table[base + 20] + pcm[firstSample + 21] * table[base + 21] +
          pcm[firstSample + 22] * table[base + 22] + pcm[firstSample + 23] * table[base + 23];
      } else {
        const phase = Math.min(PHASES - 1, Math.round((position - center) * PHASES));
        const base = phase * taps;
        let sum = 0, weightSum = 0;
        for (let k = 0; k < taps; k++) {
          const sample = firstSample + k;
          if (sample < 0 || sample >= pcmLen) continue;
          const w = table[base + k];
          sum += pcm[sample] * w;
          weightSum += w;
        }
        output[i] = weightSum > 0 ? sum / weightSum : 0;
      }
    }
    return output;
  }

  /* ------------------------------------------------------------------
     4. Source time -> timeline time
     ------------------------------------------------------------------ */
  /** clip: { in, out, speed }, clipStart: where the clip begins on the timeline. */
  function sourceToTimeline(sourceRelativeSeconds, clipStart, speed) {
    const sp = num(speed, 1) > 0 ? num(speed, 1) : 1;
    return clipStart + sourceRelativeSeconds / sp;
  }


  return {
    TIMELINE_FPS, MIN_FRAME, ACTIVITY, CAPTION,
    analyzeSpeechActivity, planCaptionChunks, planCaptionStreamWindows, buildCaptionLines, groupWordsIntoPhrases, mergeCaptionSegments, hasSpeech, resamplePcm, sourceToTimeline, stripRepeatingLoops
  };
}));
